Do not edit it here: edit\n``DATABASE.md`` and rebuild. It is hardened and tested once, in Drydock's own suite, so every\nTarget inherits a persistence layer that has already passed rather than one re-derived from prose\non each build.\n\nThe application-facing surface is ``Database``. One attribute per declared table, one method per\ndeclared access pattern::\n\n    db = Database(dsn, SCHEMA)\n    ticket = db.tickets.get(id=7)\n    open_tickets = db.tickets.list(status=\"open\")\n\n``schema.py`` beside this file is generated from ``DATABASE.md`` and carries the tables, the\noperations, and the row types. Routes are added\nonly by the feature modules that declare them.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom flask import Flask\n\nfrom app_server.books_views import bp as books_bp\nfrom app_server.settings import DATABASE_EXTENSION, PROJECT_ROOT, database_dsn\nfrom persistence import open_database\n\n__all__ = [\"create_app\"]\n\nSCHEMA_PATH = PROJECT_ROOT / \"schema.sql\"\n\n\ndef create_app() -> Flask:\n    \"\"\"Build the Flask application with its store, the blueprints, and the error handlers.\"\"\"\n    app = Flask(__name__)\n    database = open_database(database_dsn())\n    database.execute_script(SCHEMA_PATH.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n    app.extensions[DATABASE_EXTENSION] = database\n    app.register_blueprint(books_bp)\n    return app\n=== app_server/books_service.py\n\"\"\"Framework-independent book operations for the reading list.\"\"\"\n\nfrom persistence import Database\nfrom persistence.schema import Book\n\n__all__ = [\"InvalidBook\", \"add_book\"]\n\n\nclass InvalidBook(ValueError):\n    \"\"\"A submitted book was rejected; ``field`` names the empty or missing field.\"\"\"\n\n    def __init__(self, field: str) -> None:\n        super().__init__(f\"{field} must not be empty\")\n        self.field = field\n\n\ndef add_book(database: Database, title: object, author: object) -> Book:\n    \"\"\"Store a book whose title and author are non-empty after trimming, and return it with its id.\"\"\"\n    cleaned_title = _required(\"title\", title)\n    cleaned_author = _required(\"author\", author)\n    book_id = database.books.create(title=cleaned_title, author=cleaned_author)\n    return Book(id=book_id, title=cleaned_title, author=cleaned_author)\n\n\ndef _required(field: str, value: object) -> str:\n    if not isinstance(value, str) or not value.strip():\n        raise InvalidBook(field)\n    return value.strip()\n=== app_server/books_views.py\n\"\"\"HTTP surface for the book list. Thin: parse the request, call the service, shape the response.\"\"\"\n\nfrom flask import Blueprint, current_app, jsonify, request\n\nfrom app_server.books_service import InvalidBook, add_book\nfrom app_server.settings import DATABASE_EXTENSION\n\n__all__ = [\"bp\"]\n\nbp = Blueprint(\"books\", __name__)\n\n\n@bp.post(\"/books\")\ndef create_book():\n    payload = request.get_json(silent=True)\n    if not isinstance(payload, dict):\n        payload = {}\n    database = current_app.extensions[DATABASE_EXTENSION]\n    try:\n        book = add_book(database, payload.get(\"title\"), payload.get(\"author\"))\n    except InvalidBook as exc:\n        return jsonify(field=exc.field), 400\n    return jsonify(id=book.id, title=book.title, author=book.author), 201\n=== app_server/settings.py\n\"\"\"Runtime settings for the reading list, read when the application is created.\"\"\"\n\nimport os\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\n__all__ = [\"DATABASE_ENV\", \"DATABASE_EXTENSION\", \"PROJECT_ROOT\", \"database_dsn\"]\n\nPROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parent.parent\nDATABASE_ENV = \"READINGLIST_DATABASE\"\nDATABASE_EXTENSION = \"readinglist.database\"\n\n\ndef database_dsn() -> str:\n    \"\"\"Return the store named by READINGLIST_DATABASE, or the development store under data/.\"\"\"\n    configured = os.environ.get(DATABASE_ENV)\n    if configured:\n        return configured\n    return f\"sqlite://{PROJECT_ROOT / 'data' / 'app.db'}\"\n=== app_server/testing.py\n\"\"\"Test client helper shared by programmatic acceptance and the pytest fixture.\"\"\"\n\nfrom flask.testing import FlaskClient\n\nfrom app_server import create_app\n\n\ndef web_client() -> FlaskClient:\n    \"\"\"Return a test client for a fresh application with TESTING enabled.\"\"\"\n    app = create_app()\n    app.config[\"TESTING\"] = True\n    return app.test_client()\n=== persistence/__init__.py\n\"\"\"Persistence for this Target. Shipped engines plus the schema generated from DATABASE.md.\"\"\"\n\nfrom .db import Database, PersistenceError, UnknownOperation\nfrom .schema import SCHEMA, open_database\n\n__all__ = [\"Database\", \"PersistenceError\", \"SCHEMA\", \"UnknownOperation\", \"open_database\"]\n=== persistence/db.py\n\"\"\"Persistence access for a Drydock Target.\n\nThis file is shipped by Drydock and copied into the build unchanged. Do not edit it here: edit\n``DATABASE.md`` and rebuild. It is hardened and tested once, in Drydock's own suite, so every\nTarget inherits a persistence layer that has already passed rather than one re-derived from prose\non each build.\n\nThe application-facing surface is ``Database``. One attribute per declared table, one method per\ndeclared access pattern::\n\n    db = Database(dsn, SCHEMA)\n    ticket = db.tickets.get(id=7)\n    open_tickets = db.tickets.list(status=\"open\")\n\n``schema.py`` beside this file is generated from ``DATABASE.md`` and carries the tables, the\noperations, and the row types. It contains no SQL. Statements are constructed here, by the driver\nfor the engine in use, so a parameter placeholder never appears in generated code or in a caller.\n\nValues reach the database as bound parameters, never as formatted text. Identifiers cannot be\nbound in any engine, so they are taken only from the generated schema — which Drydock validated\nagainst ``^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`` before emitting it — and are quoted on the way out.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Iterable, Mapping, Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fields\nfrom typing import Any\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"Database\",\n    \"Driver\",\n    \"Operation\",\n    \"PersistenceError\",\n    \"Schema\",\n    \"Table\",\n    \"statements\",\n    \"UnknownOperation\",\n]\n\nVERBS = (\"get\", \"list\", \"create\", \"update\", \"delete\")\n\n\nclass PersistenceError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"A persistence failure the caller may act on, raised in place of a driver exception.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnknownOperation(PersistenceError):\n    \"\"\"A verb was requested that the declaration does not grant for that table.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Table:\n    \"\"\"One declared table: its columns and the column that identifies a row.\"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    columns: tuple[str, ...]\n    primary_key: str | None = None\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Operation:\n    \"\"\"One declared access pattern, reduced to what executing it requires.\n\n    ``inputs`` are the parameters the caller supplies; for ``get``, ``list``, and ``delete`` they\n    are the predicate, and for ``create`` and ``update`` they are the values written. ``row`` is\n    the dataclass a read returns, and ``columns`` are the columns it selects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    identifier: str\n    table: str\n    verb: str\n    inputs: tuple[str, ...]\n    row: type | None = None\n    columns: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    key: str | None = None\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Schema:\n    \"\"\"The generated declaration: every table and every operation reaching one.\"\"\"\n\n    tables: tuple[Table, ...]\n    operations: tuple[Operation, ...]\n\n    def by_table(self) -> dict[str, tuple[Operation, ...]]:\n        grouped: dict[str, list[Operation]] = {table.name: [] for table in self.tables}\n        for operation in self.operations:\n            grouped.setdefault(operation.table, []).append(operation)\n        return {name: tuple(items) for name, items in grouped.items()}\n\n\nclass Driver:\n    \"\"\"Statement construction shared by every engine.\n\n    Subclasses supply connection handling and the two things engines genuinely disagree about:\n    how a parameter is spelled, and how a freshly inserted row reports its key. Everything else is\n    identical SQL, so it is written once here rather than twice in the engines.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    #: Spelled by the DB-API driver, not by this class's callers.\n    paramstyle = \"qmark\"\n    returning = False\n\n    def connect(self) -> Any:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def close(self) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def placeholder(self, index: int) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Render parameter ``index`` (0-based) in this engine's spelling.\"\"\"\n        return \"?\" if self.paramstyle == \"qmark\" else \"%s\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def quote(identifier: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Quote a declared identifier. Never called with caller-supplied text.\"\"\"\n        if not identifier.replace(\"_\", \"\").isalnum():\n            raise PersistenceError(f\"refusing unsafe identifier {identifier!r}\")\n        return f'\"{identifier}\"'\n\n    def _predicate(self, names: Sequence[str], start: int = 0) -> str:\n        return \" AND \".join(\n            f\"{self.quote(name)} = {self.placeholder(start + It is hardened and tested once, in Drydock's own suite, so every\nTarget inherits a persistence layer that has already passed rather than one re-derived from prose\non each build.\n\nThe application-facing surface is ``Database``. One attribute per declared table, one method per\ndeclared access pattern::\n\n    db = Database(dsn, SCHEMA)\n    ticket = db.tickets.get(id=7)\n    open_tickets = db.tickets.list(status=\"open\")\n\n``schema.py`` beside this file is generated from ``DATABASE.md`` and carries the tables, the\noperations, and the row types. Statements are constructed here, by the driver\nfor the engine in use, so a parameter placeholder never appears in generated code or in a caller.\n\nValues reach the database as bound parameters, never as formatted text. Identifiers cannot be\nbound in any engine, so they are taken only from the generated schema — which Drydock validated\nagainst ``^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`` before emitting it — and are quoted on the way out.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nfrom collections.abc import Callable, Iterable, Mapping, Sequence\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, fields\nfrom typing import Any\n\n__all__ = [\n    \"Database\",\n    \"Driver\",\n    \"Operation\",\n    \"PersistenceError\",\n    \"Schema\",\n    \"Table\",\n    \"statements\",\n    \"UnknownOperation\",\n]\n\nVERBS = (\"get\", \"list\", \"create\", \"update\", \"delete\")\n\n\nclass PersistenceError(RuntimeError):\n    \"\"\"A persistence failure the caller may act on, raised in place of a driver exception.\"\"\"\n\n\nclass UnknownOperation(PersistenceError):\n    \"\"\"A verb was requested that the declaration does not grant for that table.\"\"\"\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Table:\n    \"\"\"One declared table: its columns and the column that identifies a row.\"\"\"\n\n    name: str\n    columns: tuple[str, ...]\n    primary_key: str | None = None\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Operation:\n    \"\"\"One declared access pattern, reduced to what executing it requires.\n\n    ``inputs`` are the parameters the caller supplies; for ``get``, ``list``, and ``delete`` they\n    are the predicate, and for ``create`` and ``update`` they are the values written. ``row`` is\n    the dataclass a read returns, and ``columns`` are the columns it selects.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    identifier: str\n    table: str\n    verb: str\n    inputs: tuple[str, ...]\n    row: type | None = None\n    columns: tuple[str, ...] = ()\n    key: str | None = None\n\n\n@dataclass(frozen=True)\nclass Schema:\n    \"\"\"The generated declaration: every table and every operation reaching one.\"\"\"\n\n    tables: tuple[Table, ...]\n    operations: tuple[Operation, ...]\n\n    def by_table(self) -> dict[str, tuple[Operation, ...]]:\n        grouped: dict[str, list[Operation]] = {table.name: [] for table in self.tables}\n        for operation in self.operations:\n            grouped.setdefault(operation.table, []).append(operation)\n        return {name: tuple(items) for name, items in grouped.items()}\n\n\nclass Driver:\n    \"\"\"Statement construction shared by every engine.\n\n    Subclasses supply connection handling and the two things engines genuinely disagree about:\n    how a parameter is spelled, and how a freshly inserted row reports its key. Everything else is\n    identical SQL, so it is written once here rather than twice in the engines.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    #: Spelled by the DB-API driver, not by this class's callers.\n    paramstyle = \"qmark\"\n    returning = False\n\n    def connect(self) -> Any:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def close(self) -> None:  # pragma: no cover - engine specific\n        raise NotImplementedError\n\n    def placeholder(self, index: int) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Render parameter ``index`` (0-based) in this engine's spelling.\"\"\"\n        return \"?\" if self.paramstyle == \"qmark\" else \"%s\"\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def quote(identifier: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Quote a declared identifier. Never called with caller-supplied text.\"\"\"\n        if not identifier.replace(\"_\", \"\").isalnum():\n            raise PersistenceError(f\"refusing unsafe identifier {identifier!r}\")\n        return f'\"{identifier}\"'\n\n    def _predicate(self, names: Sequence[str], start: int = 0) -> str:\n        return \" AND \".join(\n            f\"{self.quote(name)} = {self.placeholder(start + offset)}\"\n            for offset, name in enumerate(names)\n        )\n\n    def select(self, table: str, columns: Sequence[str], predicate: Sequence[str]) -> str:\n        selected = \", \".join(self.quote(name) for name in columns)\n        statement = f\"SELECT {selected} FROM {self.quote(table)}\"\n        if predicate:\n            statement += f\" WHERE {self._predicate(predicate)}\"\n        return statement\n\n    def insert(self, table: str, columns: Sequence[str], key: str | None) -> str:\n        names = \", \".join(self.quote(name) for name in columns)\n        marks = \", \".join(self.placeholder(index) for index in range(len(columns)))\n        statement = f\"INSERT INTO {self.quote(table)} ({names}) VALUES ({marks})\"\n        if self.returning and key:\n            statement += f\" RETURNING {self.quote(key)}\"\n        return statement\n\n    def update(self, table: str, columns: Sequence[str], predicate: Sequence[str]) -> str:\n        assignments = \", \".join(\n            f\"{self.quote(name)} = {self.placeholder(index)}\" for index, name in enumerate(columns)\n        )\n        statement = f\"UPDATE {self.quote(table)} SET {assignments}\"\n        if predicate:\n            statement += f\" WHERE {self._predicate(predicate, len(columns))}\"\n        return statement\n\n    def delete(self, table:
